Dual semidefinite programs

In this section, we detail the derivation of the dual semidefinite programs

(

D-SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

and

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

. The generalisations for

(

SDP

m
,
≥

a

)

and

(

SDP

m
,
≤

a

)

are straightforward.

A standard form for a semidefinite program is given by
[
50
]
:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{X\in\text{Sym}_{N}}{\text{sup}}\text{Tr}(C^{T}X)\\
&\text{subject to}&&\forall i\in\llbracket 1,M\rrbracket,\quad\text{Tr}(B^{(i)}X)=b_{i}\\
&\text{and }&&X\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(SDP)

where
M,N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\bm{b}=(b_{1},\dots,b_{M})\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
,
C\in\text{Sym}_{N}
, and
B^{(i)}\in\text{Sym}_{N}
for all
i\in\llbracket 1,M\rrbracket
. Its dual semidefinite program reads:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}}{\text{inf}}\bm{b}^{T}\bm{y}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{i=1}^{M}y_{i}B^{(i)}\succeq C.\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP)

D.1
Dual program for the semidefinite relaxations

We fix
m\geq n
and we recall below the expression of

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\text{Sym}_{m+1}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{n}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{k=0}^{m}F_{k}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall k\leq m,\;F_{k}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall l\leq m,\forall i+j=2l,\quad A_{ij}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}F_{k}\binom{l}{k}l!\\
&\text{and}&&\forall l\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket,\forall i+j=2l-1,\quad A_{ij}=0\\
&\text{and}&&A\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{SDP}^{m,\geq}_{n})

To put

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

in the standard form
(SDP)
we set
N=2\times(m+1)
and
M=1+(m+1)^{2}
. For all
r\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and all
i,j\in\llbracket 1,r\rrbracket
, let
E^{(i,j)}_{r}
be the
r\times r
matrix whose
(i,j)
entry is
1
and all other entries are
0
. We set

\displaystyle X=\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}(F_{k})\oplus A\in\text{Sym}_{N},

(106)

\displaystyle C=E^{(n,n)}_{N}=E^{(n,n)}_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{m+1}\in\text{Sym}_{N},

\displaystyle\bm{b}=(1,0,0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{M},

\displaystyle B^{(0)}=\mymathbb 1_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{m+1}\in\text{Sym}_{N},

\displaystyle\forall i,j\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad B^{(i,j)}=\begin{cases}\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}\left(-\binom{l}{k}l!\right)\oplus\left(\frac{1}{2}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}+\frac{1}{2}E^{(j,i)}_{m+1}\right)&\text{when }i+j=2l,\\
\mymathbb 0_{m+1}\oplus\left(\frac{1}{2}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}+\frac{1}{2}E^{(j,i)}_{m+1}\right)&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

with the convention
\binom{l}{k}=0
when
k>l
. The matrix
B^{(0)}
corresponds to the constraint
\sum_{k=0}^{m}F_{k}=1
, and we denote the corresponding dual variable
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Similarly, the matrices
B^{(i,j)}
correspond to the
(m+1)^{2}
constraints defining the symmetric matrix
A
, and we denote the corresponding dual variables
Q_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}
, with
Q_{ij}=Q_{ji}
for all
i,j\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
. We write
Q=(Q_{ij})_{0\leq i,j\leq m}
.
The standard form
(D-SDP)
of the dual program

(

D-SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

thus reads:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{m+1}\\
y\in\mathbb{R}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}\left[y-\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}\binom{l}{k}l!\right]\oplus\frac{1}{2}Q\succeq E^{(n,n)}_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{m+1}.\hskip-113.81102pt\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{n}^{m,\geq}
)

Due to the block-diagonal structure of the matrices involved, the positive semidefinite constraint above is equivalent to the following constraints:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &y\geq 1+\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}\binom{l}{n}l!,\\
&\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket\setminus\{n\},\quad y\geq\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}\binom{l}{k}l!,\\
&Q\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(107)

For
k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
, we define

\mu_{k}:=\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}\binom{l}{k}l!\in\mathbb{R}.

(108)

We obtain the program:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{m+1}\\
y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{m+1}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{n}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket\setminus\{n\},\quad y\geq\mu_{k}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad\mu_{k}=\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}\binom{l}{k}l!\hskip-56.9055pt\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{n}^{m,\geq}
)

Finally, in order to obtain the form of

(

D-SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

from the main text we prove the following result:

Lemma 9
.

Let
\bm{u},\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}
. The following propositions are equivalent:

(i)

\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad u_{k}=\sum_{l=0}^{m}v_{l}\binom{l}{k}l!
,

(ii)

\forall l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad v_{l}=\sum_{k=l}^{m}\frac{(-1)^{l+k}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}u_{k}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
8
. Note that we could start the first sum at
l=k
since
\binom{l}{k}=0
for
l<k
but for convenience we start it at
l=0
.

(i)
\Rightarrow
(ii): suppose that

\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad u_{k}=\sum_{p=0}^{m}v_{l}\binom{p}{k}p!.

(109)

Then, for all
l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{l}u_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{l}\sum_{p=0}^{m}v_{p}\binom{p}{k}p!

(110)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=0}^{m}v_{p}\frac{p!}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{m}(-1)^{k+l}\binom{p}{k}\binom{k}{l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=l}^{m}v_{p}\frac{p!}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{p}(-1)^{k+l}\binom{p}{k}\binom{k}{l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=l}^{m}v_{p}\frac{p!}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{p}(-1)^{k+l}\frac{p!k!}{k!(p-k)!l!(k-l)!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=l}^{m}v_{p}\frac{p!}{l!}\binom{p}{l}\sum_{q=0}^{p-l}(-1)^{q}\binom{p-l}{q}

\displaystyle=v_{l},

where we used Eq. (
109
) in the first line, the fact that
\binom{p}{k}=0
if
k>p
in the third line,
q:=k-l
in the fifth line, and the binomial theorem in the last line which imposes
p=l
.

(ii)
\Rightarrow
(i): suppose that

\forall l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad v_{l}=\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{p=l}^{m}(-1)^{p}\binom{p}{l}u_{p}.

(111)

Then, for all
k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{m}v_{l}\binom{l}{k}l!

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{m}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{p=l}^{m}(-1)^{p}\binom{p}{l}u_{p}\binom{l}{k}l!

(112)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=0}^{m}u_{p}(-1)^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}(-1)^{l}\binom{p}{l}\binom{l}{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=k}^{m}u_{p}(-1)^{p}\sum_{l=k}^{p}(-1)^{l}\binom{p}{l}\binom{l}{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=k}^{m}u_{p}(-1)^{p}\sum_{l=k}^{p}(-1)^{l}\frac{p!l!}{l!(p-l)!k!(l-k)!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=k}^{m}u_{p}(-1)^{p-k}\binom{p}{k}\sum_{q=0}^{p-k}(-1)^{q}\binom{p-k}{q}

\displaystyle=u_{k},

where we used Eq. (
111
) in the first line, the fact that
\binom{l}{k}=0
if
k>l
in the third line,
q:=l-k
in the fifth line, and the binomial theorem in the last line which imposes
p=k
.

∎

Combining Lemma
9
for
u_{k}=\mu_{k}
and
v_{l}=\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}
for all
k,l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
with the previous expression of
(D-SDP

n

m
,
≥

)
we finally obtain:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{m+1}\\
y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{m+1}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{n}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket\setminus\{n\},\quad y\geq\mu_{k}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}=\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{l}\mu_{k}\hskip-56.9055pt\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{n}^{m,\geq}
)

Note that the constraint
y\geq 1+\mu_{n}
implies the constraint
y\geq\mu_{n}
.

D.2
Dual program for the semidefinite restrictions

The derivation is analogous to that of the previous section.
We fix
m\geq n
and we recall below the expression of

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{m+1}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{n}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{k=0}^{m}F_{k}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall k\leq m,\;F_{k}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall l\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket,\quad\sum_{i+j=2l-1}Q_{ij}=0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall l\leq m,\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}=\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{l}F_{k}\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{SDP}^{m,\leq}_{n})

To put

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

in the standard form
(SDP)
we set
N=2\times(m+1)
and
M=1+m+(m+1)
. For all
r\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and all
i,j\in\llbracket 1,r\rrbracket
, recall that
E^{(i,j)}_{r}
denotes the
r\times r
matrix whose
(i,j)
entry is
1
and all other entries are
0
. We set

\displaystyle X=\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}(F_{k})\oplus Q\in\text{Sym}_{N},

(113)

\displaystyle C=E^{(n,n)}_{N}=E^{(n,n)}_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{m+1}\in\text{Sym}_{N},

\displaystyle\bm{b}=(1,0,0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{M},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle B^{\prime}}^{(0)}=\mymathbb 1_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{m+1}\in\text{Sym}_{N},

\displaystyle\forall l\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket,\quad{B^{\prime}}^{(l)}=\mymathbb 0_{m+1}\oplus\left(\sum_{i+j=2l-1}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}\right),

\displaystyle\forall l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad B^{(l)}=\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}\left(-\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}\right)\oplus\left(\sum_{i+j=2l}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}\right),

with the convention
\binom{k}{l}=0
when
l>k
. The matrix
{B^{\prime}}^{(0)}
corresponds to the constraint
\sum_{k=0}^{m}F_{k}=1
and we denote the corresponding dual variable
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Similarly, the matrices
{B^{\prime}}^{(l)}
correspond to the
m
constraints
\sum_{i+j=2l-1}Q_{ij}=0
, and we denote the corresponding dual variables
\nu_{l}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
. Finally, the matrices
B^{(l)}
correspond to the
m+1
constraints
\sum_{i+j=2l}Q_{ij}=\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{l}F_{k}
, and we denote the corresponding dual variables
\nu_{l}\in\mathbb{R}
.

The standard form
(D-SDP)
of the dual program

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

thus reads:

\displaystyle\hskip 91.04872pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\nu},\bm{\nu}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\\
y\in\mathbb{R}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}\left[y-\sum_{l=0}^{k}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}\right]\\
&&&\quad\oplus\left(\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l}\nu_{l}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l-1}\nu_{l}^{\prime}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}\right)\succeq E^{(n,n)}_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{m+1}.\hskip-113.81102pt\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{n}^{m,\leq}
)

Due to the block-diagonal structure of the matrices involved, the positive semidefinite constraint above is equivalent to the following constraints:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &y\geq 1+\sum_{l=0}^{n}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{n+l}}{l!}\binom{n}{l},\\
&\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket\setminus\{n\},\quad y\geq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l},\\
&\left(\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l}\nu_{l}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l-1}\nu_{l}^{\prime}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}\right)\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(114)

Let us define
A=(A_{ij})_{0\leq i,j\leq m}
by

A:=\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l}\nu_{l}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1}+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{i+j=2l-1}\nu_{l}^{\prime}E^{(i,j)}_{m+1},

(115)

or equivalently

A_{ij}=\begin{cases}\nu_{l}&\text{when }i+j=2l,\\
\nu_{l}^{\prime}&\text{when }i+j=2l-1,\end{cases}

(116)

for all
i,j\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
.
For
k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
, we also define

\mu_{k}:=\sum_{l=0}^{k}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}\in\mathbb{R}.

(117)

By Lemma
8
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad\mu_{k}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}
,

(ii)

\forall l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad\nu_{l}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}\mu_{k}\binom{l}{k}l!
.

With Eq. (
116
) we thus have

A_{ij}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}\mu_{k}\binom{l}{k}l!\quad\text{when }i+j=2l,

(118)

and we obtain the following expression for

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\text{Sym}_{m+1}\\
y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{m+1}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{n}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket\setminus\{n\},\quad y\geq\mu_{k}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall l\leq m,\forall i+j=2l,\quad A_{ij}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}\mu_{k}\binom{l}{k}l!\\
&\text{and}&&A\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{n}^{m,\leq}
)

Note that the constraint
y\geq 1+\mu_{n}
implies the constraint
y\geq\mu_{n}
.

Appendix E
Proof of Lemma
6

In this section, we prove the following result:

Lemma 6
.

For all
m\geq n
, the program

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

is equivalent to the following program:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\mathbb{R}\\
\bm{\mu}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{N})\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{n}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall k\neq n\in\mathbb{N},\;y\geq\mu_{k}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall g\in\mathcal{R}_{m,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),\;\langle f_{\bm{\mu}},g\rangle\geq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{n}^{m,\leq}
)

where
f_{\bm{\mu}}=\sum_{k}\mu_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}
.

Proof.

We first obtain a reformulation of

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

and we derive its dual program. This reformulation is obtained using Stieltjes characterisation of non-negative polynomials over
\mathbb{R}_{+}
rather than Lemma
4
:

Lemma 10

(
[
65
]
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and let
P
be a univariate polynomial of degree
m
. Let
a_{1}=\left\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\right\rfloor
and
a_{2}=\left\lfloor\frac{m-1}{2}\right\rfloor
. For all
q\in\mathbb{N}
, let
X_{q}=(1,x,\dots,x^{q})
be the vector of univariate monomials up to degree
q
. Then,
P
is non-negative over
\mathbb{R}_{+}
if and only if there exist sum of squares polynomials
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
of degree
2a_{1}
and
2a_{2}
, respectively, such that
P(x)=A_{1}(x)+xA_{2}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, or equivalently, if and only if there exist real positive semidefinite matrices
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
of size
(a_{1}+1)\times(a_{1}+1)
and
(a_{2}+1)\times(a_{2}+1)
, respectively, such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

P(x)=X_{a_{1}}^{T}A_{1}X_{a_{1}}+xX^{T}_{a_{2}}A_{2}X_{a_{2}}.

(119)

By Lemma
10
, the program

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

, obtained by imposing
F_{k}=0
for
k>m
in
(LP

n

L
2

)
, is equivalent to the following program:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A_{1},A_{2}\in\text{Sym}_{a_{1}+1}\times\text{Sym}_{a_{2}+1}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{n}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{k=0}^{m}F_{k}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall k\leq m,\;F_{k}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall l\leq m,\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{m}(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{l}F_{k}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l\\
0\leq i,j\leq a_{1}\end{subarray}}(A_{1})_{ij}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l-1\\
0\leq i,j\leq a_{2}\end{subarray}}(A_{2})_{ij}\hskip-28.45274pt\\
&\text{and}&&A_{1}\succeq 0\\
&\text{and}&&A_{2}\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{SDP}^{m,\leq}_{n})

To put

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

in the standard form
(SDP)
we set
N=(m+1)+(a_{1}+1)+(a_{2}+1)
and
M=1+(m+1)
. For all
r\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and all
i,j\in\llbracket 1,r\rrbracket
, recall that
E^{(i,j)}_{r}
denotes the
r\times r
matrix whose
(i,j)
entry is
1
and all other entries are
0
. We set

\displaystyle X=\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}(F_{k})\oplus A_{1}\oplus A_{2}\in\text{Sym}_{N},

(120)

\displaystyle C=E^{(n,n)}_{N}=E^{(n,n)}_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{a_{1}+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{a_{2}+1}\in\text{Sym}_{N},

\displaystyle\bm{b}=(1,0,0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{M},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle B}^{(-1)}=\mymathbb 1_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{a_{1}+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{a_{2}+1}\in\text{Sym}_{N},

\displaystyle\forall l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad B^{(l)}=\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}\left(-\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}\right)\oplus\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l\\
0\leq i,j\leq a_{1}\end{subarray}}E^{(i,j)}_{a_{1}+1}\right)\oplus\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l-1\\
0\leq i,j\leq a_{2}\end{subarray}}E^{(i,j)}_{a_{2}+1}\right),

with the convention
\binom{k}{l}=0
when
l>k
. The matrix
{B}^{(-1)}
corresponds to the constraint
\sum_{k=0}^{m}F_{k}=1
and we denote the corresponding dual variable
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Similarly, the matrices
B^{(l)}
correspond to the
m+1
other linear constraints, and we denote the corresponding dual variable
\bm{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}
.
The standard form
(D-SDP)
of the dual program

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

thus reads:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}\\
y\in\mathbb{R}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&\text{Diag}_{k=0,\dots,m}\left[y-\sum_{l=0}^{k}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}\right]\\
&&&\quad\oplus\left(\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l\\
0\leq i,j\leq a_{1}\end{subarray}}\nu_{l}E^{(i,j)}_{a_{1}+1}\!\right)\\
&&&\quad\oplus\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l-1\\
0\leq i,j\leq a_{2}\end{subarray}}\nu_{l}E^{(i,j)}_{a_{2}+1}\!\right)\succeq E^{(n,n)}_{m+1}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{a_{1}}\oplus\mymathbb 0_{a_{2}}.\hskip-113.81102pt\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{n}^{m,\leq}
)

Due to the block-diagonal structure of the matrices involved, the positive semidefinite constraint above is equivalent to the following constraints:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &y\geq 1+\sum_{l=0}^{n}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{n+l}}{l!}\binom{n}{l},\\
&\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket\setminus\{n\},\quad y\geq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l},\\
&\sum_{l=0}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l\\
0\leq i,j\leq a_{1}\end{subarray}}\nu_{l}E^{(i,j)}_{a_{1}+1}\succeq 0\\
&\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l-1\\
0\leq i,j\leq a_{2}\end{subarray}}\nu_{l}E^{(i,j)}_{a_{2}+1}\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(121)

For
k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
, we define

\mu_{k}:=\sum_{l=0}^{k}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}\in\mathbb{R}.

(122)

By Lemma
8
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad\mu_{k}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}\nu_{l}\frac{(-1)^{k+l}}{l!}\binom{k}{l}
,

(ii)

\forall l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad\nu_{l}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}\mu_{k}\binom{l}{k}l!
.

We thus have

\forall l\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket,\quad\nu_{l}=\sum_{k=0}^{l}\mu_{k}\binom{l}{k}l!

(123)

Let us introduce the moment matrices
M_{\nu}\in\text{Sym}_{a_{1}+1}
and
M^{(1)}_{\nu}\in\text{Sym}_{a_{2}+1}
:

\displaystyle\forall i,j\in\llbracket 0,a_{1}\rrbracket,\;

\displaystyle(M_{\nu})_{i,j}:=\nu_{i+j},

(124)

\displaystyle\forall i,j\in\llbracket 0,a_{2}\rrbracket,\;

\displaystyle(M_{\nu}^{(1)})_{ij}:=\nu_{i+j+1}.

(125)

The constraints (
121
) are equivalent to:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &y\geq 1+\mu_{n},\\
&\forall k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket\setminus\{n\},\quad y\geq\mu_{l},\\
&M_{\nu}\succeq 0\\
&M_{\nu}^{(1)}\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(126)

We complete the vector
\bm{\mu}
with zeros to obtain an element of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{N})
. We have
f_{\bm{\mu}}=\sum_{k}\mu_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
. We prove the following result, analogous to Lemma
5
:

Lemma 11
.

The following propositions are equivalent:

(i)

\forall g\in\mathcal{R}_{m,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),\;\braket{f_{\bm{\mu}},g}\geq 0
,

(ii)

M_{\nu}\succeq 0
and
M_{\nu}^{(1)}\succeq 0
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
5
:
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\mu_{k}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathcal{L}_{k}(x)f_{\bm{\mu}}(x)dx.

(127)

Thus,

\displaystyle M_{\nu}\succeq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;Y^{T}M_{\nu}Y\geq 0

(128)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;\sum_{i,j=0}^{a_{1}}y_{i}y_{j}(M_{\nu})_{ij}\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;\sum_{l=0}^{2a_{1}}\sum_{i+j=l}y_{i}y_{j}\nu_{l}\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;\sum_{l=0}^{2a_{1}}\sum_{i+j=l}y_{i}y_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{l}\mu_{k}\binom{l}{k}l!\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{l=0}^{2a_{1}}\sum_{i+j=l}y_{i}y_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{l}\binom{l}{k}l!\mathcal{L}_{k}(x)f_{\bm{\mu}}(x)dx\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{l=0}^{2a_{1}}\sum_{i+j=l}y_{i}y_{j}\sum_{k=0}^{l}(-1)^{k}\binom{l}{k}l!L_{k}(x)e^{-\frac{x}{2}}f_{\bm{\mu}}(x)dx\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\frac{x}{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{2a_{1}}x^{l}\sum_{i+j=l}y_{i}y_{j}f_{\bm{\mu}}(x)dx\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\frac{x}{2}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{a_{1}}y_{k}x^{k}\right)^{2}f_{\bm{\mu}}(x)dx\geq 0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{1}+1},\;\left\langle f_{\bm{\mu}},x\mapsto e^{-\frac{x}{2}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{a_{1}}y_{k}x^{k}\right)^{2}\right\rangle\geq 0,

where we used Eq. (
124
) in the third line, Eq. (
123
) in the fourth line, Eq. (
127
) in the fifth line and Eq. (
80
) in the seventh line. Similarly,

M_{\nu}^{(1)}\succeq 0\Leftrightarrow\forall Y\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{2}+1},\;\left\langle f_{\bm{\mu}},x\mapsto e^{-\frac{x}{2}}x\left(\sum_{k=0}^{a_{2}}y_{k}x^{k}\right)^{2}\right\rangle\geq 0.

(129)

Combining Eq. (
128
) and Eq. (
129
) with Lemma
10
we obtain

M_{\nu}\succeq 0\text{ and }M_{\nu}^{(1)}\succeq 0\Leftrightarrow\forall g\in\mathcal{R}_{m,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),\;\braket{f_{\bm{\mu}},g}\geq 0,

(130)

by linearity.

∎

Combining Lemma
11
with the constraints (
126
) finally yields:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\mathbb{R}\\
\bm{\mu}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{N})\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&\forall k\neq n\in\mathbb{N},\;y\geq\mu_{k}\\
&\text{and}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{n}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall g\in\mathcal{R}_{m,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),\;\langle f_{\bm{\mu}},g\rangle\geq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{n}^{m,\leq}
)

∎

Appendix F
Proof of Lemma
7

We recall the definition of
\bm{F}^{n}=(F_{k}^{n})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}
:

•

if
n
is even:

\displaystyle F_{k}^{n}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\binom{k}{\frac{k}{2}}\binom{n-k}{\frac{n-k}{2}}&\text{when }k\leq n,k\text{ even},\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(131)

•

if
n
is odd:

\displaystyle F_{k}^{n}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\frac{\binom{n}{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\binom{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}{\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor}^{2}}{\binom{n}{k}},&\text{when }k\leq n,\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(132)

In both cases,

F_{n}^{n}=\frac{1}{2^{n}}\binom{n}{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}.

(133)

We extrapolated these analytical expressions from numerical values. Running

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

for several values of
n
and
m
allowed us to deduce these sequences (we acknowledge here the great help from
oeis.org
).

We start by showing two results, corresponding to
n
even and
n
odd, respectively, where we make use of Zeilberger’s algorithm, a powerful algorithm for proving binomial identities
[
70
]
. Given a holonomic function, this algorithm outputs a recurrence relation that it satisfies, thus reducing the proof of identity between binomial expressions to the verification that the initialisation is correct. A Mathematica notebook is available for the implementation of Zeilberger’s algorithm
[
53
]
.

Lemma 12
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
even:

\sum_{k=0}^{n}(-1)^{k}F_{k}^{n}L_{k}(x)=\sum_{l=0}^{n}x^{l}\sum_{i+j=2n-2l}p_{n-i}p_{n-j},

(134)

where:

\displaystyle p_{n-k}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2^{n}n!}\binom{n}{\frac{n}{2}}},&\text{when }k=0,\\
(-1)^{\frac{k}{2}}2^{\frac{k}{2}}(\frac{k}{2})!\binom{\frac{n}{2}}{\frac{k}{2}}^{2}p_{n},&\text{when }0<k\leq n,\;k\text{ even },\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(135)

The coefficients
p_{n-k}
(and
q_{n-k}
later on) were found by hand when looking for an analytical sum of squares decomposition.

Proof.

To prove the polynomial equality (
134
), we start by equating the coefficients in
x^{l}
for
l\in\llbracket 0,n\rrbracket
which gives:

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{n}(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{l}F_{k}^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i+j=2n-2l}p_{n-i}p_{n-j}

(136)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2n-2l}p_{n-i}p_{n-(2n-2l-i)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n-l}p_{n-2i}p_{n-(2n-2l-2i)},

where we used
p_{n-i}=0
for
n-i<0
in the second line.
These are equalities between holonomic functions of parameters
n
and
l
that are trivial for
l>n
.

•

For
l\leq n
even, because
F^{n}_{k}=0
for
k
odd, Eq. (
136
) becomes:

\sum_{k=\frac{l}{2}}^{\frac{n}{2}}\frac{1}{l!}\binom{2k}{l}F_{2k}^{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{n-l}p_{n-2i}p_{n-(2n-2l-2i)},

(137)

that is, taking into account the parity of
l=2s
and
n=2t
,

\sum_{k=s}^{t}\frac{1}{(2s)!}\binom{2k}{2s}F_{2k}^{2t}=\sum_{i=0}^{2t-2s}p_{2t-2i}p_{2t-(4t-4s-2i)}.

(138)

Inserting the expressions from Eq. (
131
) and Eq. (
135
), we thus have to check the identity:

\sum_{k=s}^{t}\frac{1}{2^{2t}(2s)!}\binom{2k}{2s}\binom{2k}{k}\binom{2t-2k}{t-k}=\sum_{i=0}^{2t-2s}\frac{i!(2t-2s-i)!}{2^{2s}(2t)!}\binom{2t}{t}\binom{t}{i}^{2}\binom{t}{2t-2s-i}^{2},

(139)

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and all
s\leq t
(with the convention
\binom{k}{j}=0
for
j>k
).
We ran Zeilberger’s algorithm to show that the right-hand side and the left-hand side of Eq. (
138
) both satisfy the following recurrence relation, for all
s,t\in\mathbb{N}
:

2(t+1)^{2}S(s,t)+(-2s^{2}-4t^{2}+4st+5s-11t-8)S(s,t+1)+(s-t-2)(2s-2t-3)S(s,t+2)=0.

(140)

It remains to check that the initialisation is correct. For all
s\in\mathbb{N}
, this recurrence relation in
t
is of order
2
. Since the identities in Eq. (
139
) are trivial when
l>n
, i.e.,
s>t
, we thus only need to check Eq. (
139
) for
(s,t)=(0,0)
,
(s,t)=(0,1)
, and
(s,t)=(1,1)
, which is straightforward: we obtain the values
1
,
1
and
\frac{1}{4}
respectively, for both sides of Eq. (
139
).

•

For
l\leq n
odd, Eq. (
136
) becomes:

-\sum_{k=\frac{l+1}{2}}^{\frac{n}{2}}\frac{1}{l!}\binom{2k}{l}F_{2k}^{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{n-l}p_{n-2i}p_{n-(2n-2l-2i)},

(141)

that is, taking into account the parity of
l=2s+1
and
n=2t
:

-\sum_{k=s+1}^{t}\frac{1}{(2s+1)!}\binom{2k}{2s+1}F_{2k}^{2t}=\sum_{i=0}^{2t-2s-1}p_{2t-2i}p_{2t-(4t-4s-2i-2)}.

(142)

Inserting the expressions from Eq. (
131
) and Eq. (
135
), we thus have to check the identity:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=s+1}^{t}\frac{1}{2^{2t}(2s+1)!}

\displaystyle\binom{2k}{2s+1}\binom{2k}{k}\binom{2t-2k}{t-k}

(143)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2t-2s-1}\frac{i!(2t-2s-i-1)!}{2^{2s+1}(2t)!}\binom{2t}{t}\binom{t}{i}^{2}\binom{t}{2t-2s-i-1}^{2},

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and all
s\leq t
(with the convention
\binom{k}{j}=0
for
j>k
).
Likewise, we ran Zeilberger’s algorithm to show that the right-hand side and the left-hand side of Eq. (
142
) both satisfy the following recurrence relation, for all
s,t\in\mathbb{N}
:

2(t+1)^{2}S(s,t)+(-2s^{2}-4t^{2}+4st+3s-9t-6)S(s,t+1)+(s-t-1)(2s-2t-3)S(s,t+2)=0.

(144)

It remains to check that the initialisation is correct. For all
s\in\mathbb{N}
, this recurrence relation in
t
is of order
2
. Since the identities in Eq. (
143
) are trivial when
l>n
, i.e.,
s\geq t
, we thus only need to check Eq. (
143
) for
(s,t)=(0,1)
, which is straightforward: we obtain the value
1
for both sides of Eq. (
143
).

∎

Lemma 13
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
odd:

\sum_{k=0}^{n}(-1)^{k}F_{k}^{n}L_{k}(x)=\sum_{l=0}^{n}x^{l}\sum_{i+j=2l}q_{i}q_{j}

(145)

where:

\displaystyle q_{n-k}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2^{n}n!}\binom{n}{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}},&\text{when }k=0,\\
(-1)^{\frac{k}{2}}2^{\frac{k}{2}}(\frac{k}{2})!\frac{n+1}{n-k+1}\binom{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}{\frac{k}{2}}^{2}q_{n},&\text{when }0<k<n,\;k\text{ even},\\
0.&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(146)

Proof.

Unlike the case where
n
is even,
\bm{F}^{n}
is non-zero for
k\leq n
odd, and the expression of
F^{n}_{k}
depends on the parity of
k
. Thus we cannot use directly Eq. (
132
) in Zeilberger’s algorithm as we did in the previous lemma, hence the development below in order to obtain expressions that the algorithm can take as inputs. We fix
n
odd and
l\leq n
.

We start by equating coefficents in
x^{l}
in Eq. (
145
):

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\sum_{k=l}^{n}(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{l}F_{k}^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i+j=2n-2l}q_{n-i}q_{n-j}

(147)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n-l}q_{n-2i}q_{n-(2n-2l-2i)}.

•

For
l
even, writing
l=2s
and
n=2t+1
, the left-hand side of Eq. (
147
) becomes:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2s)!}\sum_{k=2s}^{2t+1}

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{2s}F_{k}^{2t+1}

(148)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2s)!}\sum_{k=0}^{2t+1-2s}(-1)^{k}\binom{k+2s}{2s}F_{k+2s}^{2t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2s)!}\sum_{k=0}^{t-s}\left(\binom{2k+2s}{2s}F_{2k+2s}^{2t+1}-\binom{2k+2s+1}{2s}F_{2k+2s+1}^{2t+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{2t+1}{t}}{2^{2t+1}(2s)!}\sum_{k=0}^{t-s}\left(\frac{\binom{2k+2s}{2s}\binom{t}{k+s}^{2}}{\binom{2t+1}{2k+2s}}-\frac{\binom{2k+2s+1}{2s}\binom{t}{k+s}^{2}}{\binom{2t+1}{2k+2s+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=q_{2t+1}^{2}\frac{(2t+1)!}{(2s)!}\sum_{k=0}^{t-s}\frac{\binom{t}{k+s}^{2}\binom{2k+2s}{2s}}{\binom{2t+1}{2k+2s}}\left(1-\frac{(2k+2s+1)^{2}}{(2k+1)(2t-2s-2k+1)}\right).

With Eq. (
146
) and Eq. (
147
), we thus have to check the identity:

\displaystyle\frac{(2t+1)!}{(2s)!}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{t-s}\frac{\binom{t}{k+s}^{2}\binom{2k+2s}{2s}}{\binom{2t+1}{2k+2s}}\left(1-\frac{(2k+2s+1)^{2}}{(2k+1)(2t-2s-2k+1)}\right)

(149)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2t+1-2s}\frac{q_{2t+1-2i}q_{2t+1-(4t+2-4s-2i)}}{q_{2t+1}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=0}^{2t+1-2s}2^{2t-2s+1}\frac{(2t+2)^{2}i!(2t+1-2s-i)!}{(2t-2i+2)(4s+2i-2t)}\binom{t}{i}^{2}\binom{t}{2t-2s-i+1}^{2},

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and all
s\leq t
(with the convention
\binom{k}{j}=0
for
j>k
).
Zeilberger’s algorithm certifies that the right-hand side and the left-hand side of Eq. (
149
) both satisfy for all
s,t\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle-32

\displaystyle(t+2)^{3}(t+1)^{2}(t+3)S(s,t)

(150)

\displaystyle+4(t+3)(t+2)(2s^{2}+4t^{2}-4st-7s+15t+14)S(s,t+1)

\displaystyle+(-2s+2t+5)(s-t-2)S(s,t+2)=0.

It remains to check that the initialisation is correct. For all
s\in\mathbb{N}
, this recurrence relation in
t
is of order
2
. Since the identities in Eq. (
149
) are trivial when
l>n
, i.e.,
s>t
, we thus only need to check Eq. (
149
) for
(s,t)=(0,0)
,
(s,t)=(0,1)
, and
(s,t)=(1,1)
, which is straightforward: we obtain the values
0
,
0
and
-8
respectively, for both sides of Eq. (
149
).

•

For
l
odd, writing
l=2s+1
and
n=2t+1
, the left-hand side of Eq. (
147
) becomes:

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{(2s+1)!}\sum_{k=2s+1}^{2t+1}

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}\binom{k}{2s+1}F_{k}^{2t+1}

(151)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{(2s+1)!}\sum_{k=0}^{2t-2s}(-1)^{k+2s+1}\binom{k+2s+1}{2s+1}F_{k+2s+1}^{2t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2s+1)!}\sum_{k=0}^{t-s}\left(-\binom{2k+2s}{2s+1}F_{2k+2s}^{2t+1}+\binom{2k+2s+1}{2s+1}F_{2k+2s+1}^{2t+1}\right)

\displaystyle=q_{2t+1}^{2}\frac{(2t+1)!}{(2s+1)!}\sum_{k=0}^{t-s}\left(-\frac{\binom{2k+2s}{2s+1}\binom{t}{k+s}^{2}}{\binom{2t+1}{2k+2s}}+\frac{\binom{2k+2s+1}{2s+1}\binom{t}{k+s}^{2}}{\binom{2t+1}{2k+2s+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=q_{2t+1}^{2}\frac{(2t+1)!}{(2s+1)!}\sum_{k=0}^{t-s}\frac{\binom{2k+2s}{2s+1}\binom{t}{k+s}^{2}}{\binom{2t+1}{2k+2s}}\left(-1+\frac{(2k+2s+1)^{2}}{2k(2t-2k-2s+1)}\right),

where we used that
\binom{2k+2s}{2s+1}=0
for
k=0
in the third line. Note that when factorising we introduced an indeterminate form in the last line that Zeilberger’s algorithm can resolve. This is necessary since the algorithm cannot deal with differences of binomial terms.
With Eq. (
146
) and Eq. (
147
), we thus have to check the identity:

\displaystyle\frac{(2t+1)!}{(2s+1)!}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{t-s}\frac{\binom{2k+2s}{2s+1}\binom{t}{k+s}^{2}}{\binom{2t+1}{2k+2s}}\left(-1+\frac{(2k+2s+1)^{2}}{2k(2t-2k-2s+1)}\right)

(152)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2t-2s}\frac{q_{2t+1-2i}q_{2t+1-(4t-4s-2i)}}{q_{2t+1}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2t-2s}2^{2t-2s}\frac{(2t+2)^{2}i!(2t-2s-i)!}{(2t-2i+2)(4s+2i-2t+2)}\binom{t}{i}^{2}\binom{t}{2t-2s-i}^{2},

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and all
s\leq t
(with the convention
\binom{k}{j}=0
for
j>k
).
Zeilberger’s algorithm certifies that both the right-hand side and the left-hand side of Eq. (
152
) satisfy for all
s\leq t
:

\displaystyle-32

\displaystyle(t+2)^{3}(t+1)^{2}(t+3)S(s,t)

(153)

\displaystyle+4(t+3)(t+2)(2s^{2}+4t^{2}-4st-5s+13t+11)S(s,t+1)

\displaystyle+(-2s+2t+3)(s-t-2)S(s,t+2)=0.

It remains to check that the initialisation is correct. For all
s\in\mathbb{N}
, this recurrence relation in
t
is of order
2
. Since the identities in Eq. (
152
) are trivial when
l>n
, i.e.,
s>t
, we thus only need to check Eq. (
152
) for
(s,t)=(0,0)
,
(s,t)=(0,1)
, and
(s,t)=(1,1)
, which is straightforward: we obtain the values
1
,
16
and
1
respectively, for both sides of Eq. (
152
).

∎

Having derived these identities, we now recall the lemma from the main text we wish to prove:

Lemma 7
.

For all
m\geq n
,
\bm{F}^{n}
is a feasible solution of

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

. Moreover, it is optimal when
m=n
.

Proof.

The proof has three parts. In the first we focus on
n
even, and in the second on
n
odd.
In the last part, we exhibit a feasible solution of

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

for
m=n
with the same optimal value
F_{n}^{n}
.

n
even,
m\geq n
:

we check that
\bm{F}^{n}
defined in Eq. (
131
) satisfies all the constraints of

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

.

•

For all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
F_{k}^{n}\geq 0
.

•

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}F_{k}^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=0\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}^{n}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\binom{k}{\frac{k}{2}}\binom{n-k}{\frac{n-k}{2}}

(154)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\frac{n}{2}}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\binom{2k}{k}\binom{n-2k}{\frac{n}{2}-k}

\displaystyle=1,

where the last equality follows from
[
69
, (3.90)]
.

•

We have to show that
x\mapsto\sum_{k=0}^{n}(-1)^{k}F_{k}^{n}L_{k}(x^{2})
is a positive function on
\mathbb{R}
.
Due to Lemma
3
, we aim to find a sum of squares decomposition for this polynomial. Guided by numerical results, we look for a polynomial
P(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{i=0}^{n}p_{i}x^{i}
such that:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}(-1)^{k}F_{k}^{n}L_{k}(x^{2})

\displaystyle=P^{2}(x^{2})

(155)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n}p_{i}x^{2i}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{n}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l\\
0\leq i,j\leq n\end{subarray}}p_{i}p_{j}\right)x^{2l},

and the sought coefficients are given by Lemma
12
, which concludes the first part of the proof.

n
odd,
m\geq n
:

similarly, we check that
\bm{F}^{n}
defined in Eq. (
132
) satisfies all the constraints of

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

.

•

For all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
F_{k}^{n}\geq 0
.

•

Writing
n=2t+1
, from Eq. (
132
) we have

\begin{split}&\forall s\leq t,\;F^{2t+1}_{2s}=\frac{1}{2^{2t+2}}\left(1-\frac{s}{t+1}\right)\binom{2s}{s}\binom{2t+2-2s}{t+1-s}\\
&\forall s\in\llbracket 1,t+1\rrbracket,\;F^{2t+1}_{2s-1}=\frac{1}{2^{2t+2}}\frac{s}{t+1}\binom{2s}{s}\binom{2t+2-2s}{t+1-s}.\end{split}

(156)

In particular, for all
s\leq t
we have

F^{2t+1}_{2s-1}+F^{2t+1}_{2s}=F^{2t+2}_{2s},

(157)

where
F^{2t+2}_{2s}
is defined in Eq. (
131
). Since
F^{2t+2}_{2s+1}=0
for all
s\leq t
, and
F_{k}^{n}=0
for all
k>n
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}F_{k}^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}F_{k}^{n+1}

(158)

\displaystyle=1,

where we used Eq. (
154
).

•

We have to show that
x\mapsto\sum_{k=0}^{n}(-1)^{k}F_{k}^{n}L_{k}(x^{2})
is a positive function on
\mathbb{R}
.
Due to Lemma
3
, we aim to find a sum of squares decomposition for this polynomial. Guided by numerical results, we look for a polynomial
Q(x)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{i=0}^{n}q_{i}x^{i}
such that:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}(-1)^{k}F_{k}^{n}L_{k}(x^{2})

\displaystyle=Q^{2}(x^{2})

(159)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n}q_{i}x^{2i}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{n}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=l\\
0\leq i,j\leq n\end{subarray}}q_{i}q_{j}\right)x^{2l},

and the sought coefficients are given by Lemma
13
, which concludes the second part of the proof.

We thus obtained a feasible solution of
(LP

n

L
2

)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, which is feasible for

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

for all
m\geq n
.

Optimality for
m=n
:

we now find an analytical solution of the dual (D-SDP
{}_{n}^{n,\leq}
) with the same optimal value as the primal (SDP
{}_{n}^{n,\leq}
), by finding a Cholesky decomposition for the matrix appearing in the dual program

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

for
m=n
. The coefficients of the Cholesky decomposition are given by the triangular matrix
L
with coefficients:

\begin{split}\forall j\leq i,\quad&l_{2i,2j}=2^{i}i!\binom{i}{j}\\
\forall j\leq i,\quad&l_{2i+1,2j+1}=2^{i+1/2}\frac{(i+1)!}{\sqrt{j+1}}\binom{i}{j}\\
&l_{nn}=0\\
&l_{ij}=0\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{split}

(160)

Once again, these analytical expressions were extrapolated from numerical values.
Then,
A=LL^{T}
is a positive semidefinite matrix given by:

A_{ij}=\sum_{k=0}^{\min(i,j)}l_{ik}l_{jk}.

(161)

Now
l_{ij}
is non-zero only when
i
and
j
have the same parity, so for all
k\in\llbracket 0,\min(i,j)\rrbracket
,
i
and
j
must have the same
parity than
k
for
l_{ik}l_{jk}
to be non-zero.

•

Suppose
i=2i^{\prime}
,
j=2j^{\prime}
,
i^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}
and
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(n,n)
. Furthermore let
l=\frac{i+j}{2}
.

\displaystyle A_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=0\\
k\text{ even}\end{subarray}}^{i}l_{2i^{\prime},k}l_{2j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}

(162)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{i^{\prime}}2^{i^{\prime}}i^{\prime}!\binom{i^{\prime}}{k}2^{j^{\prime}}j^{\prime}!\binom{j^{\prime}}{k}

\displaystyle=2^{l}i^{\prime}!(l-i^{\prime})!\sum_{k=0}^{i^{\prime}}\binom{i^{\prime}}{k}\binom{l-i^{\prime}}{k}

\displaystyle=2^{l}l!.

•

Suppose
i=2i^{\prime}+1
,
j=2j^{\prime}+1
,
i^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}
and
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(n,n)
. Furthermore let
l=\frac{i+j}{2}
.

\displaystyle A_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=0\\
k\text{ odd}\end{subarray}}^{i}l_{2i^{\prime}+1,k}l_{2j^{\prime}+1,k}

(163)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{i^{\prime}}2^{i^{\prime}+1/2}\frac{(i^{\prime}+1)!}{\sqrt{k+1}}\binom{i^{\prime}}{k}2^{j^{\prime}+1/2}\frac{(j^{\prime}+1)!}{\sqrt{k+1}}\binom{j^{\prime}}{k}

\displaystyle=2^{l}i^{\prime}!(l-i^{\prime})!\sum_{k=0}^{i^{\prime}}\binom{i^{\prime}+1}{k+1}\binom{l-i^{\prime}-1}{k}

\displaystyle=2^{l}l!.

•

Suppose
n=2t
:

\displaystyle A_{nn}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{t}l_{2t,2k}^{2}

(164)

\displaystyle=2^{2t}(t!)^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\binom{t}{k}^{2}

\displaystyle=2^{n}(t!)^{2}\left(\binom{2t}{t}-1\right)

\displaystyle=2^{n}n!\left(1-\binom{n}{\left\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\right\rfloor}^{-1}\right).

•

Suppose
n=2t+1
:

\displaystyle A_{nn}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=0\\
k\text{ odd}\end{subarray}}^{t}l_{2t+1,k}^{2}

(165)

\displaystyle=2^{2t+1}(t+1)!^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{k+1}\binom{t}{k}^{2}

\displaystyle=2^{n}t!(t+1)!\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\binom{t+1}{k+1}\binom{t}{k}

\displaystyle=2^{n}t!(t+1)!\left(\binom{2t+1}{t}-1\right)

\displaystyle=2^{n}n!\left(1-\binom{n}{\left\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\right\rfloor}^{-1}\right).

In both cases,
A
is indeed constructed as:

(A_{\bm{\mu}})_{i,j}=\begin{cases}\sum_{k=0}^{l}\mu_{k}\binom{l}{k}l!&\text{when }i+j=2l,\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(166)

for
\bm{\mu}=(F_{n}^{n},F_{n}^{n},\dots,F_{n}^{n},1-F_{n}^{n})
with
F_{n}^{n}=\frac{1}{2^{n}}\binom{n}{\left\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\right\rfloor}
, and this provides a feasible solution of

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

for
m=n
, with value
F_{n}^{n}
.
This shows the optimality of
\bm{F}^{n}
for (SDP
{}_{n}^{n,\leq}
) (and the fact that strong duality holds between the programs (SDP
{}_{n}^{n,\leq}
) and (D-SDP
{}_{n}^{n,\leq}
), which we already knew from Theorem
2
).

∎

For

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

, we see numerically that the optimal solution is the same as for (D-SDP
{}_{n}^{n,\leq}
), for a few values of
m
greater than
n
. However, this is no longer the case for higher values, for example when
n=3
and
m=10
.

Appendix G
Strong duality between
(LP

n

L
2

)
and
(D-LP

n

L
2

)

Theorem
6
shows that the optimal values of
(LP

n

L
2

)
and
(D-LP

n

L
2

)
are equal, i.e., that we have strong duality between those programs both when they are expressed in the search space
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
and
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

In this section we give another proof of strong duality between these linear programs as usually done in the literature of infinite-dimensional optimisation
[
81
,
82
]
, when they are expressed in the search space
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

Note that this result itself is not enough to provide the convergence of our hierarchy of semidefinite programs, hence the need for another proof technique: strong duality directly results from the fact the feasible set of
(LP

n

L
2

)
is closed when it is expressed over the search space
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

We use notations from Appendix
D
. From
[
61
, IV–(7.2)]
, there is no duality gap between
(LP

n

L
2

)
and
(D-LP

n

L
2

)
if there is a primal feasible plan and the cone:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\Big(A(e_{1}),\langle e_{1},c\rangle_{1}\Big):e_{1}\in K_{1}\Big\}

(167)

\displaystyle=\Big\{\Big(\sum_{k}u_{k},x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\mapsto f(x)-\sum_{k}u_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}(x),F_{n}\Big):((u_{k}),f)\in K_{1}\Big\}

is closed in
E_{2}\oplus\mathbb{R}
(for the weak topology).

Proof.

The null sequence provides a feasible plan for the primal problem.

Next, we consider a sequence
(e_{1j})_{j}=(((u_{k}^{j})_{k})_{j},(f_{j})_{j})\in K_{1}^{\mathbb{N}}=\ell^{2}(\mathbb{N})\times L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{\mathbb{N}}
11
11

11

Because we are dealing with a sequence of sequences, we use the upper index to refer to the embracing sequence.
and we want to show that the accumulation point
(b,g,a)=\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(A(e_{1j}),\langle e_{1j},c\rangle_{1})
belongs to
\mathcal{K}
where
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.

For all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
(u_{k}^{j})_{k}\in\ell^{2}
and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
u_{k}^{j}
is bounded.
Thus, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the sequence
(u_{k}^{j})_{j}
is bounded and via diagonal extraction there exists
\phi:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
strictly increasing such that
(u_{k}^{j})_{\phi(j)}
converges.
We denote
\tilde{u}_{k}
its limit. Since
\ell^{2}
is closed, the sequence
(\tilde{u}_{k})_{k}
belongs to
\ell^{2}
and we have
b=\sum_{k}\tilde{u}_{k}
and
a=\tilde{u}_{n}
.

Now
f_{j}-\sum_{k}u_{k}^{j}\mathcal{L}_{k}\longrightarrow g
so that
f_{j}\longrightarrow g+\sum_{k}\tilde{u}_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k}\in L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
since
L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
is closed.
Thus, for
\tilde{e}_{1}=((\tilde{u}_{k})_{k},g+\sum_{k}\tilde{u}_{k}\mathcal{L}_{k})\in K_{1}
,
(b,g,a)=(A(\tilde{e}_{1}),\langle\tilde{e}_{1},c\rangle_{1})
and
(b,g,a)\in\mathcal{K}
.
∎

Appendix H
Multimode case

In this section, we provide the technical background for proving the convergence of the hierarchies of semidefinite programs in the multimode setting. The results obtained are summarised in Fig.
6
.

H.1
Multimode notations and definitions

We use bold math for multi-index notations. The main advantage of these notations is that the proofs of most technical results are easily extended to the multimode setting by replacing standard notations by multi-index notations.

Let
M
denote the number of modes. We consider
M
copies
\mathcal{H}^{\otimes M}
of a separable Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
. We denote the corresponding multimode orthonormal Fock basis by
\{\ket{\bm{n}}\}_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}}
.
For all
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{M})\in\mathbb{C}^{M}
,
\bm{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
,
\bm{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we introduce the notations:

\displaystyle\bm{0}

\displaystyle=(0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{N}^{M}

(168)

\displaystyle\bm{1}

\displaystyle=(1,\dots,1)\in\mathbb{N}^{M}

\displaystyle m\bm{1}

\displaystyle=(m,\dots,m)

\displaystyle m\bm{k}

\displaystyle=(mk_{1},\dots,mk_{M})

\displaystyle\pi_{\bm{k}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{M}(k_{i}+1)

\displaystyle\hat{D}(\bm{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\hat{D}(\alpha_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{D}(\alpha_{M})

\displaystyle\ket{\bm{k}}

\displaystyle=\ket{k_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{k_{M}}

\displaystyle\bra{\bm{k}}

\displaystyle=\bra{k_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\bra{k_{M}}

\displaystyle\bm{k}\leq\bm{n}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\,k_{i}\leq n_{i}\quad\forall i=1,\dots,M

\displaystyle L_{\bm{k}}(\bm{\alpha})

\displaystyle=L_{k_{1}}(\alpha_{1})\cdots L_{k_{M}}(\alpha_{M})

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{k_{1}}(\alpha_{1})\cdots\mathcal{L}_{k_{M}}(\alpha_{M})

\displaystyle|\bm{k}|

\displaystyle=k_{1}+\cdots+k_{M}

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}^{\bm{k}}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots\alpha_{M}^{k_{M}}

\displaystyle\bm{k}!

\displaystyle=k_{1}!\cdots k_{m}!

\displaystyle\binom{\bm{n}}{\bm{k}}

\displaystyle=\binom{n_{1}}{k_{1}}\cdots\binom{n_{M}}{k_{M}}

\displaystyle\bm{k}+\bm{n}

\displaystyle=(k_{1}+n_{1},\dots,k_{M}+n_{M})

\displaystyle e^{\bm{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=e^{\alpha_{1}}\cdots e^{\alpha_{M}}.

A multivariate polynomial of degree
p\in\mathbb{N}
may then be written in the compact form
P(\bm{x})=\sum_{|\bm{l}|\leq p}p_{\bm{l}}\bm{x}^{\bm{l}}
, where the sum is over all the tuples
\bm{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
such that
|\bm{l}|\leq m
, also known as the weak compositions of the integer
m
. There are
\binom{M+m}{m}
such tuples. In what follows, we will also consider sums over all the tuples
\bm{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
such that
\bm{l}\leq\bm{k}
, for
\bm{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
. There are
\pi_{\bm{k}}
such tuples.
In particular, for all
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
and all
\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
,

L_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})=\sum_{\bm{l}\leq\bm{k}}\frac{(-1)^{|\bm{l}|}}{\bm{l}!}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}\bm{x}^{\bm{l}}\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{x}^{\bm{k}}=\sum_{\bm{l}\leq\bm{k}}(-1)^{|\bm{l}|}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}\bm{k}!L_{\bm{l}}(\bm{x}).

(169)

We extend a few definitions from the single-mode case.

For
\bm{s}=(s_{\bm{k}})_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}^{M}}
, we define the associated formal series of multivariate Laguerre functions:

f_{\bm{s}}:=\sum_{\bm{k}}s_{\bm{k}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}},

(170)

where
\bm{s}
is the so-called sequence of Laguerre moments of
f_{\bm{s}}
.
For
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we also define the associated
\binom{M+m}{m}\times\binom{M+m}{m}
matrix
A_{\bm{s}}
by

(A_{\bm{s}})_{\bm{i},\bm{j}}=\begin{cases}\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}s_{\bm{k}}\binom{\bm{l}}{\bm{k}}\bm{l}!&\text{when }\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l},\\
0&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

(171)

where
\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
with
|\bm{i}|\leq m
and
|\bm{j}|\leq m
.

The multimode Laguerre functions
(\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}})_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}}
form an orthonormal basis of the space
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
of real square-integrable functions over
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
equipped with the usual scalar product:

\braket{f,g}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}}{f(\bm{x})g(\bm{x})d\bm{x}},

(172)

for
f,g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
. We denote by
L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
its subset of non-negative elements.
The space
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
is isomorphic to its dual space
{L^{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
: elements of
{L^{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
can be identified by the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
times the corresponding function in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
.

Moreover, the elements of the space
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
of Schwartz functions over
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
, i.e., the space of
C^{\infty}
functions that go to
0
at infinity faster than any inverse polynomial, as do their derivatives, can be written as series of Laguerre functions with a sequence indexed by
\mathbb{N}^{M}
of rapidly decreasing coefficients (which go to
0
at infinity faster than any inverse
M
-variate polynomial). Its dual space
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
of tempered distributions over
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
is characterised as the space of formal series of Laguerre functions with a slowly increasing sequence indexed by
\mathbb{N}^{M}
of coefficients (sequences that are upper bounded by an
M
-variate polynomial)
[
64
]
.
We also extend the definition of the duality
\braket{\text{--},\text{--}}
in Eq. (
172
) to these spaces.

For all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the set of series of Laguerre functions over
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
truncated at
m
is denoted
\mathcal{R}_{m}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
. This is the set of
M
-variate polynomials
P(\bm{x})=\sum_{|\bm{k}|\leq m}p_{\bm{k}}\bm{x}^{\bm{k}}
of degree at most
m
multiplied by the function
\bm{x}\mapsto e^{-\frac{\bm{x}}{2}}
.
Let
\mathcal{R}_{m,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
denotes its subset of non-negative elements where the polynomial
P
is such that
\bm{x}\mapsto P(\bm{x}^{2})
has a sum-of-squares decomposition.

Similarly, for
\bm{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
, the set of truncated series of Laguerre functions over
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
with monomials smaller than
\bm{m}
is denoted
\mathcal{R}_{\bm{m}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
. This is the set of
M
-variate polynomials
P(\bm{x})=\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}p_{\bm{k}}\bm{x}^{\bm{k}}
, multiplied by the function
\bm{x}\mapsto e^{-\frac{\bm{x}}{2}}
. Let
\mathcal{R}_{\bm{m},+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
denotes its subset of non-negative elements where the polynomial
P
is such that
\bm{x}\mapsto P(\bm{x}^{2})
has a sum-of-squares decomposition.

We recall here the expressions of the linear program
(LP

n

L
2

)
and its dual
(D-LP

n

L
2

)
:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{(F_{\bm{k}})_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}}\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{N}^{M})}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{\bm{k}}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M},\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M},\quad\sum_{\bm{k}}F_{\bm{k}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})\geq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(LP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}
)

where the optimisation is over real sequences indexed by elements of
\mathbb{N}^{M}
. Its dual linear program reads

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\mathbb{R}\\
\mu\in{L^{2}}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&\forall\bm{k}\neq\bm{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{M},\;y\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}}{\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}}{d\mu}\\
&\text{and}&&y\geq 1+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}}{\mathcal{L}_{\bm{n}}}{d\mu}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall f\in L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}),\;\langle\mu,f\rangle\geq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(D-LP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}
)

We also recall the expression or
(LP

n

S

)
and
(D-LP

n

S

)
:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{(F_{\bm{k}})_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}}\in\ \mathcal{S}(\mathbb{N}^{M})}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{\bm{k}}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M},\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M},\quad\sum_{\bm{k}}F_{\bm{k}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})\geq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(LP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}}
)

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\mathbb{R}\\
\mu\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&\forall\bm{k}\neq\bm{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{M},\;y\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}}{\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}}{d\mu}\\
&\text{and}&&y\geq 1+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}}{\mathcal{L}_{\bm{n}}}{d\mu}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall f\in L^{2}_{+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}),\;\langle\mu,f\rangle\geq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(D-LP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}}
)
