Figure 6:
Multimode hierarchies of semidefinite relaxations and restrictions converging to the linear program
(LP

n

L
2

)
, together with their dual programs. The upper index
m
denotes the level of the relaxation or restriction. On the left are the associated optimal values. The equal sign denotes strong duality, i.e., equality of optimal values, and the arrows denote convergence of the corresponding sequences of optimal values. The hierarchies

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

and

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

in the main text are different from the ones appearing in the figure, but equivalent by Lemma
17
. The question of whether
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}=\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}}
is left open.

Hereafter, we state without proofs the technical results used to derive the semidefinite programs in section
6
and their dual programs. It is a straightforward exercise to obtain the proofs from their single-mode version by using multi-index notations.

There are two natural ways to obtain relaxations and restrictions by replacing constraints on non-negative functions by constraints on non-negative polynomials: either by considering polynomials
P(\bm{x})=\sum_{|\bm{k}|\leq m}p_{\bm{k}}\bm{x}^{\bm{k}}
of degree at most
m
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
, or by considering polynomials
P(\bm{x})=\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}p_{\bm{k}}\bm{x}^{\bm{k}}
with monomials smaller than
\bm{m}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
. Note that when
M=1
these two are equivalent.

All the results below containing conditions of the form
|\bm{k}|\leq m
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
are also valid when replaced by conditions of the form
\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}
for
\bm{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
, with the same proofs, by replacing
\binom{M+m}{m}
by
\pi_{\bm{m}}=\prod_{i=1}^{M}(m_{i}+1)
.

Lemma 14

(Equivalent of Lemma
3
)
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
and let
P
be a multivariate polynomial of degree
2p
. Let
\bm{X}=(\bm{x}^{\bm{k}})_{|\bm{k}|\leq p}
be the vector of monomials. Then,
P
has a sum-of-squares decomposition if and only if there exists a real
{\binom{M+p}{p}}\times{\binom{M+p}{p}}
positive semidefinite matrix
Q
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
,

P(\bm{x})=\bm{X}^{T}Q\bm{X}.

(173)

Lemma 15

(Generalisation of Lemma
4
)
.

Let
P
be a non-negative polynomial over
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
such that
\bm{x}\mapsto P(\bm{x}^{2})
has a sum-of-squares decomposition. Then,
P
can be written as a sum of polynomials of the form
\sum_{|\bm{l}|\leq p}\bm{x}^{\bm{l}}\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l}}y_{\bm{i}}y_{\bm{j}}
for
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
y_{\bm{i}}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
\bm{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
such that
|\bm{i}|\leq p
.

Lemma 16

(Generalisation of Lemma
5
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\bm{s}=(s_{\bm{k}})_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}^{M}}
. The following propositions are equivalent:

(i)

\forall g\in\mathcal{R}_{m,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}),\;\braket{f_{\bm{s}},g}\geq 0
,

(ii)

A_{\bm{s}}\succeq 0
.

Using Lemma
16
we obtain the semidefinite
relaxations
:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\text{Sym}_{\binom{M+m}{m}}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{\binom{M+m}{m}}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{|\bm{k}|\leq m}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{k}|\leq m,\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{l}|\leq m,\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l},\quad A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum\limits_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}}\binom{\bm{l}}{\bm{k}}\bm{l}!\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{r}|\leq 2m,\bm{r}\!\neq\!2\bm{l},\forall|\bm{l}|\leq m,\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=\bm{r},\;A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=0\\
&\text{and}&&A\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{SDP}^{m,\geq}_{\bm{n}})

for all
m\geq|\bm{n}|
. We denote its optimal value by
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\geq}
.
The corresponding dual programs are given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{\binom{M+m}{m}}\\
y\in\mathbb{R},\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{\binom{M+m}{m}}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{k}|\leq m,\bm{k}\neq\bm{n},\quad y\geq\mu_{\bm{k}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{l}|\leq m,\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum_{\bm{k}\geq\bm{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|\bm{k}|+|\bm{l}|}}{\bm{l}!}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}\mu_{\bm{k}}\hskip-56.9055pt\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{m,\geq}
)

for all
m\geq|\bm{n}|
. Similarly, using Lemma
14
we obtain the semidefinite
restrictions
:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{\binom{M+m}{m}}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{\binom{M+m}{m}}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{|\bm{k}|\leq m}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{k}|\leq m,\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{l}|\leq m,\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum_{\bm{k}\geq\bm{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|\bm{k}|+|\bm{l}|}}{\bm{l}!}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}}\hskip-113.81102pt\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{r}|\leq 2m,\bm{r}\neq 2\bm{l},\forall|\bm{l}|\leq m,\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=\bm{r}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=0\hskip-113.81102pt\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(SDP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{m,\leq}
)

for all
m\geq|\bm{n}|
. We denote its optimal value by
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\leq}
.
The corresponding dual programs are given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\text{Sym}_{\binom{M+m}{m}}\\
y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\binom{M+m}{m}}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{k}|\leq m,\bm{k}\neq\bm{n},\quad y\geq\mu_{\bm{k}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall|\bm{l}|\leq m,\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l},\quad A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum\limits_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}\mu_{\bm{k}}\binom{\bm{l}}{\bm{k}}\bm{l}!\\
&\text{and}&&A\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{D-SDP}^{m,\leq}_{\bm{n}})

for all
m\geq|\bm{n}|
. Like in the single-mode case, note that without loss of generality the condition
y\leq 1
, and thus
\mu_{\bm{k}}\leq 1
for all
\bm{k}
, can be added to the optimisation, since setting
A=0
,
y=1
and
\bm{\mu}=0
gives a feasible solution with objective value 1.

These are the relaxations and restrictions of
(LP

n

L
2

)
obtained by considering polynomials of degree less or equal to
m
, where the optimisation is over matrices and vectors indexed by elements of
\mathbb{N}^{m}
with sum of coefficients lower that
m
. Alternatively, we may also consider the relaxations and restrictions obtained by considering polynomials with monomials smaller than
\bm{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
, where the optimisation is over matrices and vectors indexed by elements of
\mathbb{N}^{m}
lower that
\bm{m}
. Recalling the notation
\pi_{\bm{m}}=\prod_{i=1}^{M}(m_{i}+1)
, the corresponding semidefinite
relaxations
are given by

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\text{Sym}_{\pi_{\bm{m}}}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{\bm{m}}}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq\bm{m},\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{l}\leq\bm{m},\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l},\quad A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum\limits_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}}\binom{\bm{l}}{\bm{k}}\bm{l}!\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{r}\leq 2\bm{m},\bm{r}\!\neq\!2\bm{l},\forall\bm{l}\leq\bm{m},\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=\bm{r},\;A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=0\\
&\text{and}&&A\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{SDP}^{\bm{m},\geq}_{\bm{n}})

for all
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
. We denote its optimal value by
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\bm{m},\geq}
.
The corresponding dual programs are given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{\pi_{\bm{m}}}\\
y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{\bm{m}}}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq\bm{m},\bm{k}\neq\bm{n},\quad y\geq\mu_{\bm{k}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{l}\leq\bm{m},\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum_{\bm{k}\geq\bm{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|\bm{k}|+|\bm{l}|}}{\bm{l}!}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}\mu_{\bm{k}}\hskip-56.9055pt\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{\bm{m},\geq}
)

for all
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
.
Similarly, the semidefinite
restrictions
are given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{\pi_{\bm{m}}}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{\bm{m}}}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq\bm{m},\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{l}\leq\bm{m},\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum_{\bm{k}\geq\bm{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|\bm{k}|+|\bm{l}|}}{\bm{l}!}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}}\hskip-113.81102pt\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{r}\leq 2\bm{m},\bm{r}\neq 2\bm{l},\forall\bm{l}\leq\bm{m},\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=\bm{r}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=0\hskip-113.81102pt\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(SDP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{\bm{m},\leq}
)

for all
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
. We denote its optimal value by
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\bm{m},\leq}
.
The corresponding dual programs are given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\text{Sym}_{\pi_{\bm{m}}}\\
y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{\bm{m}}}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq\bm{m},\bm{k}\neq\bm{n},\quad y\geq\mu_{\bm{k}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{l}\leq\bm{m},\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l},\quad A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum\limits_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}\mu_{\bm{k}}\binom{\bm{l}}{\bm{k}}\bm{l}!\\
&\text{and}&&A\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{D-SDP}^{\bm{m},\leq}_{\bm{n}})

for all
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
.

The programs

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

and
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
respectively provide hierarchies of relaxations and restrictions of
(LP

n

L
2

)
, since the set of
M
-variate polynomials of degree
m
is included in the set of
M
-variate polynomials of degree
m+1
.
On the other hand, there is no natural ordering in
\mathbb{N}^{M}
of the relaxations

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

or the restrictions
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
(consider for instance
\bm{m}=(2,1)
and
\bm{m}^{\prime}=(1,2)
). In order to obtain proper hierarchies of semidefinite programs, we thus consider the subset of these programs where the tuple
\bm{m}
is of the form
m\bm{1}=(m,\dots,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
, for
m\in\mathbb{N}
. We have
\pi_{m\bm{1}}=(m+1)^{M}
, and the
relaxations
are then given by

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\text{Sym}_{(m+1)^{M}}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{(m+1)^{M}}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{\bm{k}\leq m\bm{1}}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq m\bm{1},\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{l}\leq m\bm{1},\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l},\quad A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum\limits_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}}\binom{\bm{l}}{\bm{k}}\bm{l}!\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{r}\leq 2m\bm{1},\bm{r}\!\neq\!2\bm{l},\forall\bm{l}\leq m\bm{1},\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=\bm{r},\;A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=0\\
&\text{and}&&A\succeq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{SDP}^{m\bm{1},\geq}_{\bm{n}})

for
m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}
. We denote its optimal value by
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\geq}
.
The corresponding dual programs are given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{(m+1)^{M}}\\
y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{(m+1)^{M}}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq m\bm{1},\bm{k}\neq\bm{n},\quad y\geq\mu_{\bm{k}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{l}\leq m\bm{1},\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum_{\bm{k}\geq\bm{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|\bm{k}|+|\bm{l}|}}{\bm{l}!}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}\mu_{\bm{k}}\hskip-56.9055pt\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(D-SDP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\geq}
)

for
m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}
.
Similarly, the
restrictions
are given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\text{Sym}_{(m+1)^{M}}\\
\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{(m+1)^{M}}\end{subarray}}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{\bm{k}\leq m\bm{1}}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq m\bm{1},\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{l}\leq m\bm{1},\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum_{\bm{k}\geq\bm{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|\bm{k}|+|\bm{l}|}}{\bm{l}!}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}}\hskip-113.81102pt\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{r}\leq 2m\bm{1},\bm{r}\neq 2\bm{l},\forall\bm{l}\leq m\bm{1},\quad\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=\bm{r}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=0\hskip-113.81102pt\\
&\text{and}&&Q\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(SDP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\leq}
)

for
m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}
. We denote its optimal value by
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\leq}
.
The corresponding dual programs are given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\text{Sym}_{(m+1)^{M}}\\
y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{(m+1)^{M}}\end{subarray}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq m\bm{1},\bm{k}\neq\bm{n},\quad y\geq\mu_{\bm{k}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{l}\leq m\bm{1},\forall\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l},\quad A_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\sum\limits_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}\mu_{\bm{k}}\binom{\bm{l}}{\bm{k}}\bm{l}!\\
&\text{and}&&A\succeq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{D-SDP}^{m\bm{1},\leq}_{\bm{n}})

for
m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}
.

The programs

(

SDP

⁢
m
1

,
≥

n

)

and
(SDP

n

⁢
m
1

,
≤

)
are the relaxations and restrictions of
(LP

n

L
2

)
obtained by considering polynomials of individual degree in each variable less or equal to
m
. These programs respectively provide hierarchies of relaxations and restrictions of
(LP

n

L
2

)
, since the set of
M
-variate polynomials with monomials lower than
m\bm{1}
is included in the set of
M
-variate polynomials with monomials lower than
(m+1)\bm{1}
.

Note that these hierarchies of programs obtained by setting
\bm{m}
of the form
m\bm{1}
capture the behaviour of all bounds that can be obtained from the more general family of programs indexed by
\bm{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{M})
, since
\bm{m}\leq(\max_{i}m_{i})\bm{1}
, i.e., the bound obtained by considering the program indexed by
(\max_{i}m_{i})\bm{1}
supersedes the bound obtained by considering the program indexed by
\bm{m}
. Formally, for all
\bm{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
,

\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\bm{m},\geq}\geq\omega_{\bm{n}}^{(\max_{i}m_{i})\bm{1},\geq}\quad\text{and}\quad\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\bm{m},\leq}\leq\omega_{\bm{n}}^{(\max_{i}m_{i})\bm{1},\leq}.

(174)

Finally, we show that both ways of defining the hierarchies are equivalent:

Lemma 17
.

For all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\geq}\geq\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\geq}\geq\omega_{\bm{n}}^{Mm,\geq},

(175)

and

\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\leq}\leq\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\leq}\leq\omega_{\bm{n}}^{Mm,\leq}.

(176)

Proof.

For all
\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
and all
m\in\mathbb{N}
we have

|\bm{k}|\leq m\quad\Rightarrow\quad\bm{k}\leq m\bm{1}=(m,\dots,m)\quad\Rightarrow\quad|\bm{k}|\leq Mm.

(177)

We thus obtain the corresponding inclusions between sets of
M
-variate polynomials:

(i)

M
-variate polynomials of degree less or equal to
m
have all their monomials lower than
m\bm{1}
, and

(ii)

all
M
-variate polynomials with monomials lower than
m\bm{1}
have degree less or equal to
Mm
.

Hence,

\mathcal{R}_{m,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})\overset{(\text{i})}{\subset}\mathcal{R}_{m\bm{1},+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})\overset{(\text{ii})}{\subset}\mathcal{R}_{Mm,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}).

(178)

In particular,

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

is a relaxation of

(

SDP

⁢
m
1

,
≥

n

)

which is itself a relaxation of
(\text{SDP}_{\bm{n}}^{Mm,\geq})
, and
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
is a restriction of
(SDP

n

⁢
m
1

,
≤

)
which is itself a restriction of
(\text{SDP}_{\bm{n}}^{Mm,\leq})
.
∎

This result implies that the two versions of the hierarchies of relaxations are interleaved (Eq. (
175
)), and that the two versions of the hierarchies of restrictions are also interleaved (Eq. (
176
)). As such, for any bound obtained with one version of the hierarchy at some fixed level, a better bound can be obtained with the other version at some other level. While this means that the hierarchies are equivalent, note that in practice it may be simpler to solve numerically the version where the parameter space is smaller.

H.3
Convergence of the multimode hierarchies

For
\bm{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
, the sequences
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\geq})_{m\geq|\bm{n}|}
and
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\geq})_{m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}}
(resp.
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\leq})_{m\geq|\bm{n}|}
and
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\leq})_{m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}}
) are decreasing (resp. increasing) sequences, lower bounded (resp. upper bounded) by
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}
(resp.
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}})
). Hence, these sequences are converging.

We show in what follows that
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\geq})_{m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}}
(resp.
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m\bm{1},\leq})_{m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}}
) converges to
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}
(resp.
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}}
). By Lemma
17
, this implies that the sequence
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{Mm,\geq})_{m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}}
(resp.
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{Mm,\leq})_{m\geq\max_{i}n_{i}}
) also converges to
\omega_{\bm{n}}
. Since this is a subsequence of the converging sequence
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\geq})_{m\geq|\bm{n}|}
(resp.
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\leq})_{m\geq|\bm{n}|}
), it implies that the sequence
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\geq})_{m\geq|\bm{n}|}
(resp.
(\omega_{\bm{n}}^{m,\leq})_{m\geq|\bm{n}|}
) also converges to
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}
(resp.
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}}
).

With similar proofs to the single-mode case using multi-index notations, we obtain the following result:

Theorem 8

(Generalisation of Theorem
1
)
.

Let
\bm{\mu}=(\mu_{\bm{k}})_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}^{M}}
. Then,
\bm{\mu}
is the sequence of Laguerre moments
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}(\bm{x})d\mu(\bm{x})
of a non-negative distribution
\mu
supported on
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}
if and only if
\forall m\in\mathbb{N},\forall g\in\mathcal{R}_{m,+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}),\;\braket{f_{\bm{\mu}},g}\geq 0
.

The proof of this theorem is identical to the univariate case, with the use of Riesz–Haviland theorem over
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}

[
67
]
rather than
\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

With Eq. (
178
), the proof of convergence of the multimode hierarchy of upper bounds is then obtained directly from its single-mode counterpart using multi-index notations:

Theorem 9

(Generalisation of Theorem
4
)
.

The decreasing sequence of optimal values
\omega^{m\bm{1},\geq}_{\bm{n}}
of

(

SDP

⁢
m
1

,
≥

n

)

converges to the optimal value
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}
of
(LP

n

L
2

)
:

\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\omega^{m\bm{1},\geq}_{\bm{n}}=\omega_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}.

(179)

With Lemma
17
, we also obtain

\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\omega^{m,\geq}_{\bm{n}}=\omega_{\bm{n}}^{L^{2}}.

(180)

On the other hand, the proof of convergence of the single-mode hierarchy of lower bounds crucially exploits analytical feasible solutions of the programs

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

in order to obtain two results:

•

Strong duality between programs

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

and

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

(Theorem
2
).

•

The fact that the feasible set of

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

is compact with coefficients bounded independently of
m
(Eq. (
63
)).

In what follows, we generalise these two results to the multimode setting by obtaining multimode analytical feasible solutions from products of single-mode ones.

Lemma 18
.

For
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
with
m\geq n
, let
Q(m,n)\in\text{Sym}_{m+1}
and
\bm{F}(m,n)=(F_{k}(m,n))_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}
be feasible solutions of

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

. Let
\bm{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
and
\bm{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
with
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
. Let
Q:=Q(m_{1},n_{1})\otimes\dots\otimes Q(m_{M},n_{M})\in\text{Sym}_{\pi_{\bm{m}}}
and
\bm{F}=(F_{\bm{k}})_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{\bm{m}}}
, where for all
\bm{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{M})\leq\bm{m}
,
F_{\bm{k}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{M}F_{k_{i}}(m_{i},n_{i})
. Then,
(Q,\bm{F})
is a feasible solution of
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
. Moreover, if
(Q(m_{i},n_{i}),\bm{F}(m_{i},n_{i}))
is strictly feasible for all
i=1,\dots,M
then
(Q,\bm{F})
is a strictly feasible solution of
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
.

Proof.

With the notations of the Lemma, we show the feasibility of
(Q,\bm{F})
(resp. strict feasibility).
We have
Q\succeq 0
,
F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0
(resp.
Q\succ 0
,
F_{\bm{k}}>0
) for all
\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}
, and
Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=\prod_{p=1}^{M}Q_{i_{p}j_{p}}(m_{p},n_{p})
for all
\bm{i}=(i_{1},\dots,i_{M})\leq\bm{m}
and
\bm{j}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{M})\leq\bm{m}
. Hence, for all
\bm{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{M})\leq 2\bm{m}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=\bm{r}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}+j_{1}=r_{1},\dots,i_{M}+j_{M}=r_{M}}\prod_{p=1}^{M}Q_{i_{p}j_{p}}(m_{p},n_{p})

(181)

\displaystyle=\prod_{p=1}^{M}\sum_{i_{p}+j_{p}=r_{p}}Q_{i_{p}j_{p}}(m_{p},n_{p}).

In particular, if
\bm{r}\neq 2\bm{l}
for all
\bm{l}\leq\bm{m}
, then at least one coefficient
r_{p}
is odd, and the corresponding sum gives
0
since
(Q(m_{p},n_{p}),\bm{F}(m_{p},n_{p}))
is feasible for
(\text{SDP}_{n_{p}}^{m_{p},\leq})
. In that case,
\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=\bm{r}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}=0
. Otherwise, for all
\bm{l}=(l_{1},\dots,l_{M})\leq\bm{m}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{i}+\bm{j}=2\bm{l}}Q_{\bm{i}\bm{j}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{p=1}^{M}\sum_{i_{p}+j_{p}=2l_{p}}Q_{i_{p}j_{p}}(m_{p},n_{p})

(182)

\displaystyle=\prod_{p=1}^{M}\sum_{k_{p}\geq l_{p}}\frac{(-1)^{k_{p}+l_{p}}}{l_{p}!}\binom{k_{p}}{l_{p}}F_{k_{p}}(m_{p},n_{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l_{1}\leq k_{1}\leq m_{1},\dots,l_{M}\leq k_{M}\leq m_{M}}\prod_{p=1}^{M}\frac{(-1)^{k_{p}+l_{p}}}{l_{p}!}\binom{k_{p}}{l_{p}}F_{k_{p}}(m_{p},n_{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{k}\geq\bm{l}}\frac{(-1)^{|\bm{k}|+|\bm{l}|}}{\bm{l}!}\binom{\bm{k}}{\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}},

where we used the feasibility of
(Q(m_{p},n_{p}),\bm{F}(m_{p},n_{p}))
in the second line. Finally,

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}F_{\bm{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}\leq m_{1},\dots,k_{M}\leq m_{M}}\prod_{i=1}^{M}F_{k_{i}}(m_{i},n_{i})

(183)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{k_{i}=0}^{m_{i}}F_{k_{i}}(m_{i},n_{i})

\displaystyle=1,

since
\sum_{k=0}^{m}F_{k}(m,n)=1
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
with
m\geq n
. This shows that
(Q,\bm{F})
is a feasible solution of
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
(resp. strictly feasible).
∎

A direct consequence of this construction is the following result:

Theorem 10

(Generalisation of Theorem
2
)
.

Strong duality holds between the programs
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
and

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

.

Proof.

The proof of Theorem
2
gives a strictly feasible solution
(Q(m,n),\bm{F}(m,n))
of

(

SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

for all
m\geq n
. By Lemma
18
, the program
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
thus has a strictly feasible solution, for all
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
. By Slater condition, this implies that strong duality holds between the programs
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
and

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

.
∎

In particular, strong duality holds between the programs
(SDP

n

⁢
m
1

,
≤

)
and

(

D-SDP

⁢
m
1

,
≤

n

)

. Note that the multimode generalisation of Theorem
3
is a direct consequence of Theorem
10
:

Theorem 11

(Generalisation of Theorem
3
)
.

Strong duality holds between the programs

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

and
(D-SDP

n

m
,
≥

)
.

Proof.

the strictly feasible solution of
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
derived in the proof of Theorem
10
yields a strictly feasible solution for

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

.
With Slater condition, this shows again that strong duality holds between the programs

(

SDP

m
,
≥

n

)

and
(D-SDP

n

m
,
≥

)
.
∎

In particular, strong duality holds between the programs

(

SDP

⁢
m
1

,
≥

n

)

and
(D-SDP

n

⁢
m
1

,
≥

)
.

We recall the following definition from the main text: for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\bm{F}^{n}=(F_{k}^{n})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}
where

\bullet
if
n
is even:

F_{k}^{n}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\binom{k}{\frac{k}{2}}\binom{n-k}{\frac{n-k}{2}}&\text{when }k\leq n,k\text{ even},\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(184)

\bullet
if
n
is odd:

F_{k}^{n}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\frac{\binom{n}{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}\binom{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}{\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\rfloor}^{2}}{\binom{n}{k}},&\text{when }k\leq n,\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(185)

Let us define, for all
\bm{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
,
\bm{F}^{\bm{n}}=(F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{n}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{M}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}^{M}}
where

F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{n}}:=\begin{cases}\prod_{i=1}^{M}F_{k_{i}}^{n_{i}}&\text{when }\bm{k}\leq\bm{n},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(186)

By (
60
), for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
F_{n}^{n}\geq\frac{1}{n+1}
, so for all
\bm{n}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
,

F_{\bm{n}}^{\bm{n}}\geq\frac{1}{\pi_{\bm{n}}}.

(187)

Like in the single-mode case, the program
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{\bm{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{\bm{m}}}}{\text{sup}}F_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{subject to}&&\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}F_{\bm{k}}=1\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq\bm{m},\quad F_{\bm{k}}\geq 0\\
&\text{and}&&\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}F_{\bm{k}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}\in\mathcal{R}_{\bm{m},+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}),\end{aligned}\right.

(SDP
{}_{\bm{n}}^{\bm{m},\leq}
)

with the dual program given by

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &&&\underset{y,\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{\bm{m}}}}{\text{inf}}y\\
&\text{subject to}&&y\geq 1+\mu_{\bm{n}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall\bm{k}\leq\bm{m},\bm{k}\neq\bm{n},\quad y\geq\mu_{\bm{k}}\\
&\text{and}&&\forall g\in\mathcal{R}_{\bm{m},+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M}),\left\langle\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}\mu_{\bm{k}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}},g\right\rangle\geq 0,\end{aligned}\right.

(\text{D-SDP}^{\bm{m},\leq}_{\bm{n}})

for all
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
. Moreover, adding the condition
\mu_{\bm{k}}\leq 1
for all
\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}
does not change the optimal value of the program. We enforce this condition in what follows.
With Lemma
7
and Lemma
18
, we thus obtain the following result:

Lemma 19

(Generalisation of Lemma
7
)
.

For all
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
,
\bm{F}^{\bm{m}}
(defined in Eq. (
186
)) is a feasible solution of
(SDP

n

m
,
≤

)
.

In particular, for all
\bm{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
,
\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{m}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}\in\mathcal{R}_{\bm{m},+}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{M})
. For
\bm{m},\bm{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
with
\bm{m}\geq\bm{n}
, let
\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{\pi_{\bm{m}}}
be a feasible solution of

(

D-SDP

m
,
≤

n

)

. Then, for all
\bm{l}\leq\bm{m}

\left\langle\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{m}}\mu_{\bm{k}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}},\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{l}}\mathcal{L}_{\bm{k}}\right\rangle\geq 0,

(188)

so that

\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}\mu_{\bm{k}}F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{l}}\geq 0.

(189)

Hence, for all
\bm{l}\geq\bm{m}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{\bm{l}}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1}{F_{\bm{l}}^{\bm{l}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}\\
\bm{k}\neq\bm{l}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\bm{k}}F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{l}}

(190)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1}{F_{\bm{l}}^{\bm{l}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}\\
\bm{k}\neq\bm{l}\end{subarray}}F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{l}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{F_{\bm{l}}^{\bm{l}}}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\pi_{\bm{l}},

where we used
F_{\bm{l}}^{\bm{l}}>0
in the first line,
\mu_{\bm{k}}\leq 1
and
F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{l}}\geq 0
in the second line,
\sum_{\bm{k}\leq\bm{l}}F_{\bm{k}}^{\bm{l}}=1
in the third line, and Eq. (
187
) in the last line.

With these additional results, the proof of convergence of the multimode hierarchy of lower bounds
(SDP

n

⁢
m
1

,
≤

)
is then obtained directly from its single-mode counterpart using multi-index notations:

Theorem 12

(Generalisation of Theorem
5
)
.

The increasing sequence of optimal values
\omega^{m\bm{1},\leq}_{\bm{n}}
of
(SDP

n

⁢
m
1

,
≤

)
converges to the optimal value
\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}}
of
(LP

n

S

)
:

\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\omega^{m\bm{1},\leq}_{\bm{n}}=\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}}.

(191)

With Lemma
17
, we also obtain

\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\omega^{m,\leq}_{\bm{n}}=\omega_{\bm{n}}^{\mathcal{S}}.

(192)

Like in the single-mode case, Theorem
9
and Theorem
12
imply strong duality between the linear programs:

Theorem 13

(Generalisation of Theorem
6
)
.

Strong duality holds between the programs
(LP

n

L
2

)
and
(D-LP

n

L
2

)
and between programs
(LP

n

S

)
and
(D-LP

n

S

)
.
